ORDER 

New Delhi, the 31st December, 1973 

S.O. 400. — Whereas the Central Government is of opinion 
that an industrial dispute exists between the employers in 
relation to management of Dalmia Dadri Cement Limited, 
Charkhi Dadri and Shri Khub Chand Quarry Contractor of 
Messrs. Dalmia Dadri Cement Limited and their workmen 
in respect of the matters specified in the Schedule hereto 
annexed ; 

And whereas the Central Government considers it desir- 
able to refer the said dispute for adjudication; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by 
section 7A and clause (d) of sub-section (1) of Section 10 
of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), the Central 
Government hereby constitutes an Industrial Tribunal with 
Shri O. P. Shanma as Presiding Officer with headquarters at 
Faridabad and refers the said dispute for adjudication to the 
said Industrial Tribunal. 

SCHEDULE 

Whether the action pf the management of Dalmia Dadri 
Cement Limited Charkhi Dadri and of Shri Khub 
Chand Quarry Contractor of Messrs. Dalmia Dadri 
Cement Limited, Charkhi Dadri in retrenching Shri 
Darya Singh excavator in Khatiwas Quarry of Messrs 
Dalmia Dadri Cement Limited, Charkhi Dadri is 
legal and justified ? If not, to what relief is the 
workman entitled ? 

[No. L-29012/24/73-LRTV}

W. T^-29011/64/73-TvT. m-4] 
ORDER 

New Delhi, the 21st January, 1974 

S.O. 401. — Whereas the Central Government is of opinion 
that an industrial dispute exists between the employers in 
relation to the management of Messrs. Timble Private Limit- 
ed and their workmen in respect of the matters specified in 
the Schedule hereto annexed; 

And whereas the Central Government considers it desirable 
to refer the said dispute for adjudication; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by 
clause (d) of sub-section (1) of section 10 of the Industrial 
Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), the Central Government 
hereby refers the said dispute for adjudication to the Central 
Government Industrial Tribunal (No. 1), Bombay constituted 
under section 7A of the said Act. 

SCHEDULE 

Whether the management of Messrs. Timble Private 
Limited was justified in dismissing 83 workmen

S.O. 403 In pursuance of section 17 of the Industrial Dis- 
pates Act, 194"r (14 of 1947), the Central Government hereby 
publishes the following award of the Arbitrator, in the indus- 
trial dispute between the management of Kundara Clay Mines 
of Messrs Kerala Ceramics Limited, and their workmen repre- 
sented by Ceramic Workers Association, Kundara and four 
others which was received by the Central Oovcmment on 18th 
January, 1974, 

AWARD 

(Under Section 10 A of the I.D. Act — 

Act XIV of 1947). 

IN THE INDUSTRIAL DISPUTE 
BETWEEN 

Kundara Clay Mines of 
M/S. Kerala Ceramics Ltd., Kundara. 

AND 

ITS WORKMEN 
Represented by 

(i) Ceramic Workers Association. 

(ii) Ceramic Workers Union. 

(iii) Tiles and Ceramic Workers’ Congress. 

(iv) Kerala Ceramic Employees Federation, 

(v) Kerala Government Ceramic Mining Workers Union. 

By 

ARBITRATOR 

Shri. K.H. BALASUBRAMON1AM, 

Joint Labour Commissioner, 

Kerala State, Trivandum. 

PARTIES AND REPRESENTATIONS 

For the Management By Shri, T. C. K. Pillai, 

General Manager, 

Kerala Ceramics Ltd,, 
Kundara. 

By Shri T.A. Majeed, 

Mines Manager, The Kerala 
Ceramics Ltd,, Kundara. 


For the Workmen By Shri A. Bhargavan, 

Secretary, Ceramic Workers 
Association. 

By Shri, A, Joseph, Vice- 

President, Ceramic Workers 
Union. 

By Shri, T. Joseph, Secretary, 

Tiles and Ceramic Workers 
Congress. 

By Shri. G. Thankappan Pillai, 
Secretary, Kerala Ceramics 
Employees Federation. 

By Shri. A. Alocious, Secretary, 
Kerala Government Ceramic 
Mining Workers Union. 

The Government of India in their Notification No. L. 2901 1 
(35)/71-LR-IV dated 30-6-1972 have referred the following dis- 
pute between the Management of Kundara Clay Mines of M/s. 
Kerala Ceramics Ltd., Kundara and their workmen represented 
by the following LTnions for my arbitration Secretary, Ceramic 
Workers Association, Vice-President, Ceramic Workers Union, 
Secretary, Tiles and Ceramic Workers Congress, Secretary, 
Kerala Ceramics Employees Federation, Secretary, Kerala 
Government Ceramic Mining Workers Union. The Specific 
matters referred for decision are — 

(1) Whether the Managements’ action in laying off the 
workers of Kundara Clay Mines from 3-2-1971 to 6-2-197 1 
was necessary and justificable. 

(2) If so, how and in what manner should the amounts paid 
as advance for the lay off periods and amounts paid on 
8-2-1971 be recovered. 

As per the said notification written statements were obtained 
from all the parties concerned. 

The case was posted for evidence of parties on 15-12-1972, 
31-3-1973, 4-5-1973, 26-5-1973, 20-6-1973, 10-7-1973, 6-8-1973 
31-8-1973 and 20-9-1973. During the hearing on 20-9-1973 both 
the parties submit that the issue at dispute has been compro- 
mised. The management submits that while maintaining that 
the lay off was justified and necessary, they arc prepared to 
waive the recovery of the advance as a gesture of goodwill. 

The Union expressed their concurrence to the offer of Ma- 
nagement and assured against repetition of such acts. These 
assurances are recoreded and as such there is no subsisting 
dispute. 

This Award shall become enforceable on the expiry of thirty 
days from the date of its publication Under section 17 of the 
Industrial Disputes Act. 

Ordered accordingly, 

Dated at Trivandrum this day the 1 1th of October 1973. 

[No. L-29011/35/71-LR.TV] 
K.H. BALASUBRAMONIAM, 
Arbitrator (Joint Labour Commissioner)

S.O. 404. — Whereas the Central Government having been 
satisfied that the public interest so required had, in pur- 
suance of the provisions of sub-clause (vi) of clause (n) ol 
section 2 of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947) 
declared by the notification of the Government of India in 
the then Ministry of Labour and Rehabilitation (Depart- 
ment of Labour and Employment) No. S. O. 2241 dated the 
26th July, 1973 service in the Pyrites Mining Industry, to 
be a public utility service for the purposes of the said Act, 
for a period of six months from the 22nd August, 1973. 

And whereas the Central Government is of opinion that 
public interest requires extension of the said period by a 
further period of six months; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by 
the proviso to sub-clause (vi) of clause (n) of section 2 of 
the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), the Central 
Government hereby declares the said service to be a public 
utility service for the purposes of the said Act for a further 
period of six months from the 22nd February, 1974.

New Delhi, the 30th January, 1974 

S.O. 406.— In pursuance of section 17 of the Industrial 
Disputes Act, 1947, (14 of 1947) the Central Government 
hereby publishes the following award of the Central Gov- 
ernment Industrial Tribunal No. 2, Bombay, in the Industrial 
dispute between the employers in relation to management 
of Private Motor Launch Services, Bombay and their work- 
men, which was received by the Central Government on 
the 18th January, 1974. 

BEFORE THE CENTRAL GOVERNMENT INDUS- 
TRIAL TRIBUNAL NO. 2, BOMBAY 

Reference No. CGIT-2/8 of 1971 

EMPLOYERS IN RELATION TO THE MANAGEMENT 
OF PRIVATE MOTOR LAUNCH SERVICES, BOMBAY 

AND 

THEIR WORKMEN 

Present : 

Shri N. K. Vani, Presiding Officer 

Appearance* t 

For the Employer* — Shri C. H. Bhedwar, Managing 
Director. 

For the Workmen — Shri D. Y. Khclkar, General Se- 
cretary, Motor Launch Employees Association, 
Bombay. 

Industry : Ports and Docks, State : Maharashtra 

Bombay, dated the 3rd January, 1974 

AWARD 

PART-II 

By Order No. L. 31011/4/71-P&D dated 28-10-1971 the 
Government of India, in the Ministry of Labour and Reha- 
bilitation (Department of I.abour and Employment) in exer- 
cise of the powers conferred by clause (d) of sub-section (1) 
of Section 10 of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 
1947) referred to this Tribunal for adjudication an industrial 
dispute existing between the employers in relation to the 
managements of Private Motor Launch Services, Bombay 
mentioned in Schedule I and their workmen in respect of 
the matters mentioned in Schedule II as mentioned below : — 

“SCHEDULE-I 

1. Messrs Bombay Harbour Transport Company, 

Jeevan Jyoti, 1st Floor, Cawasjee Patel St., Bom- 
bay-1. 

2. Messrs B. S, Toscano, Jeevan Jyoti, 1st Floor, Cawas- 

jee Patel St., Bombay-1. 

3. Messrs Bharat Launch Service, Jeevan Jyot, 1st 

Floor, Cawasjee Patel St., Bombay-1. 

4. Messrs United Launch Service, Jeevan Jyot, 1st 

Floor, Cawasjee Patel St., Bombay-1. 

5. Messrs Vensto Launch Service, Jeevan Jyot, 1st 

Floor, Cawasjee Patel St„ Bombay-1. 



572 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : FEBRUARY 9 , 1974/MAGHA 20, 1895 [Part II— 


6. Messrs Bombay launch Service Private Limited, 
Ballard Pier Steps, Mole Station, Alexandra Dock, 
Bombay-1." 

“SCHEDULE-II 

Keeping in view the recommendations of the Central 
Wage Board for Port and Dock Workers, what 
should be the scales of pay and other service bene- 
fits for the employees of the Motor Launches of 
the employers mentioned in the Schcdulc-l above 
in the Bombay Port and Docks and from what 
date ?” 

2. On the receipt of the reference, notices were issued to 
the six employers and the Union. 

3. In pursuance of these notices, employers mentioned at 
S. No. 1 to 3, 5 and 6 Hied their respective written state- 
ments, The Union also filed its written statement. 

4. In this case I have given Part I award in respect of 
the dispute between the employers mentioned at S. No. 3 
to 5 and their workmen on 28-11-1973 us mentioned 
below ; — 

“ORDER 

(i) Award Part I in terms of Ex. 42 /EW in respect of 
employe! s mentioned at S. No. 1 to 3 and 5 in 
Schedule I and their employees is made. 

(iil There is no award in respect of Messrs United 
Launch Service mentioned at S. No. 4 in the Sche- 
dule 1 as it is not in existence. 

(iii) Settlement Ex. 42 /EW is to form part of the 
award. 

(iv) Reference be fixed for further hearing in respect 
of Messrs Bombay Launch Service Private Limi- 
ted mentioned at S. No. 6 in Schedule I and its 
employees. 

(v) Further orders are reserved.” 

5. After giving Award Part I this reference was fixed for 
hearing in respect of the dispute between the employers 
Messrs Bombay Launch Service Private Limited and its 
employees. 

6. After taking some adjournment and entering into nego- 
tiations, there was settlement between Messrs Bombay 
Launch Service Private Limited and its workmen on 1-1-1974. 

7. On 1-1-1974 Shri C. H. Bhcdwar, Managing Director, 
Bombay Launch Services and Shri D. Y. Kelkar, Secretary, 
Motor Launch Employees Association have given applica- 
tion Ex. 48/EW alongwith settlement Ex. 49 /EW praying 
that award be passed in terms of settlement, 

8. Shri C. H. Bhedwar, Managing Director of the 
Bombay Launch Service Private Limited, Ex. 50/E, Shri 
D. Y. Kelkar, Secretary, Motor Launch Employees Associa- 
tion, Ex. 51 /E, Shri J. K. Desai, Personal Officer, A, B. 
Cursetjee & Sons Pvt. Ltd., Ex. 52/E, Shri S. J. Mukadam 
Driver, Ex. 53 /W admits their signatures on the settlement 
Ex. 49 /EW. S/shri J/K. Desai and S. J. Mukadam had 
signed the settlement as witnesses. S/shri Bhedwar and Kel- 
kar signed the settlement on behalf of the employer and 
workmen respectively. They speak about the terms of 
settlement and pray that award be passed in terms of settle- 
ment. 

9. By this settlement Ex. 49 /EW the employers agreed 
to pay interim relief to the employees as mentioned in 
para. 2(2) of the settlement. They also agreed to give 
different types of leave as mentioned in para. 2(3) of the 
settlement, They agreed that the settlement shall come 
into force with effect from 1-1-1972 and that the arrears due 
to the employees shall be paid within three months from 
the date of signing the settlement. 

10. It is clear from the terms of settlement and the nature 
of dispute between the parties that this settlement is fair, 
and in the interests of the parties. It is also clear from the 
record that the settlement is voluntary and mutual. I, 
therefore accept this settlement and pass award Part II in 
terms of it. 

11. In the end I pass the following order. 


ORDER 

(i) Award Part II in terms of settlement Ex. 49 /EW in 

respect of the employers Messrs Bombay Launch 
Service Private Limited mentioned at S, No. 6 in 
Schedule I and its workmen is made. 

(ii) Settlement Ex. 49/EW is to form part of this 
Award. 

(iii) No order as to costs. 

N, K. VANI, Presiding Officer 
(Ex. 49/EW) 

MEMORANDUM OF SETTLEMENT 
PARTIES TO THE DISPUTE 

1, Shri C, H. Bhedwar Managing Director of Messrs, 

Bombay Launch Service Private Limited, Bombay. 
— Representing Employers. 

2. Shri D. Y. Kelkar General Secretary, Motor Launch 

Employees’ Association — Representing Workmen 

SHORT RECITAL 

The parties mentioned above whereas agreed to discuss 
the matter pending before the Hon’ble Tribunal under 
CGIT/8 of 1971 in the matter of Revision of Scales of Pay 
and Service Benefits, etc., to the Motor Launch Employees 
reached (he following settlement on the following terms 
after prolonged mutual discussions. 

2. Terms of settlement 

(1) It is agreed between the parties that whenever there 

is any Wage Board or any other Body set up by 
the Government for the consideration of the ser- 
vice conditions including the Scales of pay of the 
dock workers, both the parties will be bound to 
make representations before the said body and the 
decision of it will be binding on both the parties. 

(2) The employers agree to nay an interim relief to 

the employees at the following rates to different 
categories of workmen. This interim relief shall 
be treated as part of basic pay for all purposes : 

Khalasi, Lascar, Peon and other Allied categories 

Rs. 25 p.m. 

Oilmen, Greaser, Seacunny and other Allied categories 

Rs. 30 p.m. 

Driver, Sarang, Clerk and other Allied Categories 

Rs. 50 p.m. 

(3) The employers agree to grant 20 days casual leave, 

7 days sick leave and 30 days privilege leave to 
every employee instead of 21 days as at present 
provided that the increased leave is taken when 
the launch is laid up for survey. The employers 
further agree that should any employee desire to 
encash privilege leave due to him and not availed 
of by him, he will be allowed to do so. 

(4) This agreement shall come into force , with effect 
from January 1, 1972. 

(5) Tlie arrears due to the employees shall be paid 

by the employers within three months from the 
date of signing of this agreement. 

(6) This agreement shall remain in force for Two years. 
Signature of Parties 

S/Shri 

1. C. H. Bhedwar sd/- 

2, D. Y. Kelkar sd/- 

WTTNESS : 

(1) sd/- 

(2) sd/- 

Bombay, 1st January, 1974.

New Delhi, the 29 January, 1974 

S.O.408— In pursuance of sub-section (5) of section (5) 
of the Coal Mines Labour Welfare Fund Act, 1947 (32 of 1947), 
the Central Government hereby publishes the following esti- 
mates of receipts into and expenditure from the General Welfare 
Account of the Coal Mines Labour Housing and General Wel- 
fare Fund during the year 1973-74 together with a statement of 
the account for the year 1972-73 and a report on the activities 
financed during the year 1972-73 from the General Welfare 
Account of the said Fund, namely: — 

PART I 

Estimates of Receipt and Expenditure during 1973-74 
General Welfare Account 

Receipt Expenditure 

Rs. 3,53,79,000 *Rs. 3,26,40,000 

‘Provision includes Rs. 1 ,00,000 being ‘Loan' to the Colliery 
Owners for raising the standards of dispensary services. 
This also includes Rs. 16,40,000 under ‘Suspense’ which 
has at present been shown in the details of recoveries ad- 
justed in accounts in reduction of expenditure in the De- 
mands for Grants (1973-74) to accommodate transactions 
relating to stock and store after the close of the financial 
year.

N.B: The figures shown above are provisional as the account 
for 1972-73 has not yet been closed by the Accountant 
General, Bihar. 

PART III 

1. Medical Faculties. 

(a) Hospitals : The 3 Central Hospitals, one each at Dhan- 
bad, Asansol and Manendragarh and 12 Regional Hospitals 
situated indifferent Coalfields continued to function. The bed 
strength of the Central Hospitals at Dhanbad, Asansol and 
Manendragarh is 300, 350 and 50 respectively. The question 
of establishment of a Regional Hopsital in Chanda Coalfield 
and provision of a specialised ward with (he National Coal 
Development Corporation’s Hospital in Talchar Coalfield was 
under active consideration, 

(b) Dispensaries: One allopathic Static Dispensary in Mugma 
Coalfield, one Mobile Medical Unit in Assam Coalfield and 29 
Ayurvedic Dispensaries in various Coalfields continued func- 
tioning. The Ayurvedic Pharmacy set up at Patherdih in 
Jharla Coalfields to manufacture the required ayurvedic medi- 
cines also continued to function. 

(c) Family Welfare and Maternity and Child Welfare Centres: 
A Family Welfare Centre attached to each of the Regional Hos- 
pitals of the Organisation remained in operation. Besides, 
8 such centres already established by the Organisation in various 
coalfield areas also continued to function as independent units 
under the charge of qualified Lady Health Visitors. For the 
benefit of the colliery workers in the Chanda coalfield, the block 
attached to the Government Hospital, Chanda, continued to 
function. 

(d) Financial assistance for improving Dispensary Services: 

With a view to encouraging the colliery management for improv- 
ing the standard of dispensary at the collieries for the benefit 
of colliery workers and theii dependents, the scheme for the pay- 
ment of grant-in-aid was continued. A sum of Rs. 7,28,41 1 . 52 
was paid to the colliery owners during the year foi providing 
medical facilities to the colliery workers. In order to encourage 
the colliery owners to provide more dispensary services for the 
benefit of their workers, the scheme of payment of financial 
assistance was also continued. A sum of Rs, 12,03,453.05 
was paid to the colliery owners during the year. 

(e) Antl-T.B. Measures : For the treatment of colliery 
workers and their dependents suffering from T.B. besides provi- 
sion of 312 beds at the Organisation’s own T.B, Hospitals, 
61 beds remained reserved for them in different T.B, Sanatoria. 
The Scheme of Domiciliary T.B. Treatment continued to func- 
tion and payment of subsistence allowance to the T.B. patients 
undergoing treatment under the Scheme also continued to be 
made according to schedule. A sum of Rs. 7,67,994/- was 
incurred on this Scheme during the period. For reservation of 
beds, a sum of Rs. 1,04,732/- was paid during the year to the 
various sanatorium authorities. 

(f) Family Planning Services : Free advice on family plan- 
ning continued to be given and contraceptive supplied free of 
cost at the Coal Mines Labour Welfare Fund's Hospitals and 
at the clinics opened for the purpose in various coalfields. The 


scheme for the payment of grant-in-aid to the colliery owners 
for the family planning work continued to be in force. The 
5 Static Family Planning Units and 3 Mobile Family Planning 
U ms remained in operation. 

(g) Rehabilitation : The Rehabilitation Centres attached 
to each of tire Central Hospitals at Dhanbad and Asansol conti- 
nued to function. The Rehabilitation Centre at Chhindwara in 
Madhya Pradesh Coalfield was inaugurated and started function- 
ing, The question of commissioning the Rehabilitation-cum- 
Convalscent Home at Sidhabari in West Bengal Coalfield re- 
mained under active consideration, 

th) Other Medical Activities: Other important activities 
of the Organisation on the Medical and Public Health side 
were: Provision of facilities for the treatment of Cancer, Leprosy 
and Mental Cases, running of Blood BaDk at the Central Hospi- 
tals, Dhanbad and Asansol, free supply of spectacles and den- 
tures to the workers, Malaria and Filaria control operation etc. 
etc. 


2. Educational and Recreational Facilities: 

(a) Some relevant statistics high-lighting the important 


activities on this account are given below : — 

(i) Multipurpose Institutes ... 60 

(ii) Adult Education Centres ... 60 

(iii) Women Welfare Centres .... 61 

(iv) Feeder Adult Education Centres . . 152 

(v) Scholarship to the childien of Colliery 

workers . .... 546 

Cvi) Boarding Houses for school going children . 2 

(vii) Holiday Home? 2 


(b) Games and Sports : Games and sports are held every 
year to provide recreation to colliery workers. During the 
year, for organising various games and sports a Sum of Rs. 
44,000/- was spent by the Organisation. 

(c) Other Activities: Other important facilities provided 
in this connection comprise (U grant-in-aid to the educational 
Institutions, (2) provision for games and sport? amongst coal 
miners, (3) exhibition of films, (4) ({rant of tuition fees and chil- 
dren education allowance, (5) p ovision for financial assistance 
to the wives and school-going children of colliery workers who 
die of accident in mines etc. etc, 

3. Water Supply: 

During the year, a sum of Rs. 9,19,593/- has been spent on 
water supply schemes. The organisation continued to pay grant- 
in-aid as prescribed in the Water Supply Scheme up to 50% 
on the total cost of scrutinised expenditure, as the case may be, 
to the colliery managements and other bodies for implementation 
of water supply schemes in the coalfield areas. 

Sinking of Wells: No wells, under the scheme of 50% sub- 
sidy, were sunk. Total number of wells so far completed is 
266. A sum of Rs. 22,318/- was paid as subsidy to the colliery 
managements for sinking of wells in different coalfields during 
the year. 


4. Cooperative Movement: 

No new Primary Stores, Credit Co-operative Societies and 
Wholesale/Centra! Co-operative Stores were opened during 
the year, The total number of co-operative Stores and Credit 
Societies of colliery workers at the end of the period under re- 
view stood at 574. There were also 12 Wholesale or Central 
Co-operative Stores sponsored by the Organisation. 

[No. Z. 1 60 1 6/4/7 3-M.II] 

B.K. SAKSLNA, 
Under Secy,

New Delhi, the 25th January, 1974 

S.O. 409. — Tn exercise of the powers conferred by sub- 
section (1) of section 13 of the Employees' Provident Funds 
and Family Pension Fund Act, 1952 (19 of 1952), the Central 
Government hereby appoint Shri P. G. Shetty to be an Ins- 
pector for the whole of the State of Andhra Pradesh for the 
purposes of the said Act, and the Scheme and the family 
pension Scheme framed thereunder in relation to any estab- 
lishment belonging to, or under the control of the Central 
Government or in relation to any establishment connected 
with a railway company, a major port, a mine or an oilfield 
or a controlled industry or in relation to an establishment 
having departments or branches in more than one State.

New Delhi, the 29th January, 1974 

S.O. 410. — Whereas the Central Government is satisfied 
that the employees of the Posts and Telegraphs Motor Service 


[Part II — 

Workshop, Bombay, belonging to the Government of India 
in the Department of Communications, Posts and Telegraphs 
Board, are otherwise in receipt of benefits substantially simi- 
lar to the benefits provided under the Employees' State 
Insurance Act, 1948 (34 of 1948) ; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by 
section 90 of the said Act and in continuation of the noti- 
fication of the Government of India in the Ministry of 
Labour and Rehabilitation (Department of Labour and 
Employment) No. S.O. 1726, dated the 5th May, 1972 the 
Central Government, after consultation with the Employees’ 
State Insurance Corporation, hereby exempts the above men- 
tioned factory from the operation of the said Act for a 
further period of one year with effect from the 15th January, 
1973 upto and inclusive of the 14th January, 1974.

S.O. 411 . — Whereas the State Government of Punjab has, 
in pursuance of clause (d) of section 4 of the Employees’ 
State Insurance Act, 1948 (34 of 1948), nominated Shxl 
Joginder Singh, Secretary to the Government of Punjab, 
Health and Family Planning Department to represent the 
Slate of Punjab on the Employees’ State Insurance Corpora- 
tion established under sub-section (1) of section 3 of the 
said Act, in place of Shrl S. S. Parmar;

Now, therefore, in pursuance of section 4 of the Employees’ 
State Insurance Act, 1948 (34 of 1948), the Central Govern- 
ment hereby makes the following further amendment in the 
notification of the Government of India in the late Ministry 
of Labour, Employment and Rehabilitation No. S.O. 2763, 
dated the 27th May, 1971, namely:— 

In the said notification, under the heading "(Nominated 
by State Governments under clause (d) of section 4)", for 
the entry against item 19, the following entry shall be sub- 
stituted namely : — 

"Shri Joginder Singh, 

Secretary to the Government of Punjab, 

Health and Family Planning Department, Chandi- 
garh”.
